## Contract Concerns Kept Separate from SOLID Violations

- `perception/perception/compute_depth.py:32–37`: timing state/accessor is indented under the PIL AttributeError branch, yet depth_estimation imports that accessor unconditionally. This is a version-sensitive initialization/contract concern, not evidence of a subtype violating LSP. The architectural lifecycle issue is already perception/V-01.
- Both KITTI dataset implementations unpack four values from load_path (`:18`), while the two-column branch returns three (`:28`). That is a local API inconsistency for that input, not proof of a Dataset substitution-contract violation. No dataset execution was performed.
- `benchmark_evaluator/benchmark_evaluator/optuna_optimizer.py:9` uses `from benchmark_evaluator import evaluate_map_accuracy`; the package __init__.py is empty. Direct script execution can resolve the sibling module differently from package import. The diagram records intended evaluation use; installed-module execution is not verified.
- Alternative C++ A* can relocate occupied endpoints (`active_inspection_planner/src/search_algo.cpp:85`); active Python A* has a different endpoint rejection policy. These implementations have no declared common substitutability contract. Define that contract before consolidation; do not label the difference an established LSP violation.

These observations are diagnostic only; none was fixed. Binary model weights, training data and third-party installed library implementations were not semantically audited.


### FILE: docs/architecture/clean_architecture_audit_summary.md
# Clean Architecture Global System Audit

Read [Phase 1: current and target topology, metric definitions and tables](phase_1_topology.md) first. Then follow the seven package reports below. Scope is `NBV_paper_ros2/src`; all paths in findings are relative to that directory. This is an analysis-only snapshot dated 2026-10-05. No application source or configuration was modified.

## 1. Executive Summary

The system has recognizable ROS deployment components but no explicit policy-owned abstract interfaces. Policy and framework/IO code coexist inside major nodes and mapper classes. The most consequential dependency is production planning/perception importing a concrete logger from the experiment component, whose optimizer depends back on application launch/configuration.

- **Overall System Distance from Main Sequence:** mean D = **0.702381**, population variance D = **0.090986**, over seven packages under the explicitly imputed zero-denominator convention. These are unweighted package statistics, not defect probabilities or class-weighted scores.
- **Mathematically defined subset:** mean D = **0.750000**, population variance D = **0.050000**, n=5. Excludes isolated active_inspection_planner (undefined I) and class-free bringup (undefined A). An unqualified seven-package mean is mathematically undefined without a convention.
- **44 indexed findings:** 7 Critical, 34 Moderate, 3 Low. Several rows describe facets of the same architectural cause; this is not a count of independent defects.
- **Three elementary deployment-component cycles**, one increasing-I edge, and **no detected import-only inter-package cycle**. All 37 locally defined production classes/structs are non-abstract under the stated counting rule. Framework subclasses and mixin-like helpers are not counted as explicit interfaces.
- **Hotspots:** benchmark_evaluator (shared concrete dependency plus reverse orchestration); python_planner.NBVUtilityNode (mission/mapping/IO in one node); active_inspection_planner.RealTimeStereoMapper (mapping/planning/storage in one class); motion_control.WaypointControlNode (guidance/platform allocation/ROS); perception.compute_depth (import-time model/device/file initialization).

The package metrics are deliberately a closed seven-component view. Concrete outer adapters such as sim_bridge can look maximally painful in that view despite having appropriate framework dependencies. Do not rank remediation by D alone or add abstractions to stable value objects just to lower D. Positive boundaries already exist: pure route/collision/continuity functions, separate C++ search helpers, simulator factory injection and an NBV timing callback.

| Package report | Scope |
|---|---|
| [active_inspection_planner](packages/active_inspection_planner_audit.md) | Alternative C++ mapping and mission pipeline |
| [benchmark_evaluator](packages/benchmark_evaluator_audit.md) | Shared telemetry, evaluation, reporting and experiment orchestration |
| [bringup](packages/bringup_audit.md) | Composition and configuration |
| [motion_control](packages/motion_control_audit.md) | Control, waypoint queue, platform command encoding and legacy code |
| [perception](packages/perception_audit.md) | Depth estimation and vendored BGNet modules |
| [python_planner](packages/python_planner_audit.md) | Active NBV mission pipeline and legacy mapping modules |
| [sim_bridge](packages/sim_bridge_audit.md) | Simulator session, sensor adapters and environment policy |

## 2. Dependency Rule & Layering Violations

These are the distinct evidenced boundary leaks, consolidated from the package tables. Framework usage by an outer adapter is legitimate; violations arise where high-level decisions directly own the details or a data contract exposes them.

| Policy/client | Concrete outward dependency | Evidence / finding |
|---|---|---|
| C++ map/mission policy | ColorOcTree, cv::Mat, PCL traversal | [active V-01/V-02](packages/active_inspection_planner_audit.md#v-01) |
| C++ mapper and mission lifecycle | Map file persistence, stdout, node-owned metric files and fixed paths | [active V-03/V-04](packages/active_inspection_planner_audit.md#v-03) |
| Python mission state machine | ROS Node, executor callbacks, concrete OctoMap and file handles | [planner V-01](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-01) |
| NBV candidate/scoring algorithm | rclpy logging and Open3D preprocessing objects | [planner V-02](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-02) |
| Production planner and depth clients | Concrete benchmark MidrunLogger and its result-file conventions | [planner V-03](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-03), [perception V-04](packages/perception_audit.md#v-04) |
| Legacy Python mapping/planning | Viewer, Notes files, map storage and ambiguous repository Tools imports | [planner V-04/V-05](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-04), [motion V-05](packages/motion_control_audit.md#v-05) |
| Trajectory optimization acceptance policy | Fixed SciPy optimizer invocation | [planner V-07](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-07) |
| Waypoint/control policy | ROS state/timers and eight-thruster platform mixing | [motion V-01/V-03](packages/motion_control_audit.md#v-01) |
| Depth computation lifecycle | Global BGNet model, GPU selection, checkpoint filesystem and absolute imports | [perception V-01/V-02](packages/perception_audit.md#v-01) |
| Synchronized depth use case | ROS callback owns concrete inference, calibration arguments and persistence | [perception V-03](packages/perception_audit.md#v-03) |
| Evaluation metrics | ROS lifecycle, map/ground-truth loading, plotting and text/CSV output | [benchmark V-04/V-05](packages/benchmark_evaluator_audit.md#v-04) |
| Experiment objective | Shell/process groups, exact bringup config schema and result text parsing | [benchmark V-06/V-07](packages/benchmark_evaluator_audit.md#v-06) |
| Trajectory / vehicle-command contract | Untyped flattened ROS arrays and simulator-specific index layouts cross policy boundaries | [planner V-08](packages/python_planner_audit.md#v-08), [motion V-04](packages/motion_control_audit.md#v-04), [bridge V-03](packages/sim_bridge_audit.md#v-03) |
| Simulation environmental policy | Sensor loop computes vortex and applies it to hardcoded simulator agent | [bridge V-05](packages/sim_bridge_audit.md#v-05) |

The bridge's normal HoloOcean calls, bringup's launch classes, and BGNet's Torch internals belong at the outer boundary and are not independently classified as DIP failures. Plain numerical-library use does not by itself prove a Clean Architecture violation; the replaceable optimizer and preprocessing boundary findings concern policy coupling to specific mechanisms.

## 3. Circular Dependencies (ADP Violations)

Observed edges include imports, executable package-name references and direct cross-package configuration access. The exact elementary cycles, modulo starting point, are:

1. `benchmark_evaluator → bringup → benchmark_evaluator`.
2. `benchmark_evaluator → bringup → perception → benchmark_evaluator`.
3. `benchmark_evaluator → bringup → python_planner → benchmark_evaluator`.

The strongly connected component is `{benchmark_evaluator, bringup, perception, python_planner}`. `motion_control` and `sim_bridge` are outgoing sinks; `active_inspection_planner` is isolated in this scoped component graph. See the [edge evidence table](phase_1_topology.md#current-inter-package-dependency-graph).

The import-only and scoped manifest-only graph is acyclic. Cyclic ROS command/observation flow is not evidence of ADP. The optimizer is not started by the default launch; its reverse launch dependency still belongs to the shipped component's source/deployment responsibilities. The SDP violation is `benchmark_evaluator (I=0.250) → bringup (I=0.833)`. No external-library SDP claim is made because those libraries' incoming clients were not audited.

## 4. Master Violation Index

Severity: **Critical** = shared cyclic dependency or central policy/IO coupling with multiple change actors; **Moderate** = localized dependency, extension, contract or release-closure problem; **Low** = dormant-path or peripheral composition issue. Severity is architectural change impact, not a runtime safety certification. IDs are package-local; the qualified package/ID is globally unique.

## 5. Target Migration Roadmap (Analysis Only)

The current graph is cyclic, so a global dependency-depth order does not exist until the experiment/telemetry SCC is broken. Work inside that SCC first. Establish contract/domain leaves before moving policy; then attach concrete leaf adapters to those contracts. Do not begin by replacing the simulator or motor behavior.

| Order | Component boundary and dependency rationale | Reviewable completion criterion for a future refactor |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Define policy-owned value contracts and narrow telemetry/map/control ports. These become inward graph leaves; retain ROS array conversion at adapters. | Pose, trajectory, calibration, occupancy and event semantics are explicit; policy contract modules import no ROS, Torch, OctoMap or file IO. |
| 2 | Separate MidrunLogger/file sinks from evaluation and extract the optimizer into an outer experiment component. Rewire planner/perception to client-owned telemetry ports. | Recompute the component graph: all three current cycles disappear; bringup does not depend on experiment orchestration; installed component manifests describe actual edges. |
| 3 | Move class-independent domain routines first: route/collision rules, continuity costs, coordinate transforms and metric functions. Their limited dependencies make them inexpensive foundation leaves. | Existing numerical behavior and collision rejection cases remain characterized; no ROS startup or model import required. |
| 4 | Extract Python mission use cases from NBVUtilityNode; introduce map read/write/snapshot and trajectory-output adapters. Split preprocessing and solver invocation from selection/acceptance policy. | Use cases run against in-memory map/output/clock implementations; stale-pose reconnection, blacklisting, cancellation, float32 boundary checks and collision acceptance remain specified. |
| 5 | Extract C++ mapper responsibilities behind matching semantic contracts; retain its separate algorithm variant until behavior differences are measured. | Map backend replacement does not modify candidate/route policy; search and smoothing can be invoked outside ROS. |
| 6 | Split depth policy from model initialization and ROS synchronization; package model assets and isolate research/dataset utilities. | Importing depth policy performs no checkpoint/GPU/file initialization; runtime supplies estimator, calibration and timing sink explicitly. |
| 7 | Extract waypoint queue and control law, leaving platform mixing and command publication in adapters. Separate simulator sensor conversion, stepping, current-field policy and configuration loading. | Existing command semantics are characterized before changes; controller policy accepts values and substitutes output/mixer; bridge startup no longer executes a mission in its constructor. |
| 8 | Consolidate evaluation/reporting adapters and version TrialResult/configuration contracts; reconcile all seven manifests and classify legacy paths for separate release or later retirement. | Each reusable component declares its dependency closure; report wording changes do not break experiment result parsing. |

Steps 4–7 can proceed independently once shared contract ownership and SCC removal are settled. Do not migrate by deleting old paths before comparing the active Python, alternative C++ and legacy behavior. No deletion, build-system change, test alteration, controller change or implementation refactor is authorized by this audit.

## 6. Coverage, Evidence Quality and Limits

- [Source coverage and dependency/symbol inventory](source_coverage.md) records all 92 scoped text source/build/configuration files, including all 74 source/script files, tests, empty package markers, dormant code and vendored BGNet. Binary weights, images, bytecode, dataset filename lists and Markdown are not executable source and do not enter class metrics.
- Python ASTs were parsed without importing application modules; source imports, class definitions, functions, local call relationships and explicit IO/framework calls were inspected. C++ definitions/includes, build inputs, contracts and algorithm/node call sites were inspected statically; no compiler-resolved whole-program call graph is claimed. Filesystem checks and SHA-256 snapshots verify the audit did not alter these inputs.
- Functional tests, ROS nodes, GPU inference, simulator and optimizer were not run. Executing them is unnecessary for documentation generation and could create runtime artifacts or start missions. Existing tests are evidence of intended behavior, not evidence they currently pass. In particular, `python_planner/test/test_nbv_filter_logging.py:35` extracts AST function bodies and supplies replacement dependencies, supporting the need for accessible policy seams.
- LSP was evaluated for Node, Dataset and Torch Module hierarchies. No sufficiently established subtype-contract violation is asserted. Constructor blocking, arity defects and missing import symbols are not automatically LSP violations. ISP is supported by the concrete aggregate logger's clients, not by an invented abstract interface. No rule is forced to have a positive finding.
- Actor/change ownership and REP/CCP conclusions are source-based inferences; release history, actual team ownership, external component source and deployment environments were not supplied. Findings are the evidenced static issues, not a proof that no additional behavioral or organizational violation exists.
- Mermaid blocks were structurally checked for complete fences and source relationships; this audit does not claim visual rendering by a Mermaid engine.
- Documentation validation passed: 10 Markdown files, 55 Mermaid blocks, 167 local links/anchors, 44 indexed findings and unchanged SHA-256 hashes for all 92 source/build/configuration inputs. The target graph's 26 edges were checked: zero cycles and zero increasing-I edges under the same direct-neighbor counting rule.

Principle references: Robert C. Martin, [The Clean Architecture](https://blog.cleancoder.com/uncle-bob/2012/08/13/the-clean-architecture.html), [Granularity](https://objectmentor.com/resources/articles/granularity.pdf), and [Stability](https://objectmentor.com/resources/articles/stability.pdf). Numerical thresholds and undefined-denominator conventions are explicitly this audit's choices.

# Package Architecture Audit: sim_bridge

## Current Metrics

- Fan-in: 1 | Fan-out: 0
- Instability (I): 0.000
- Abstractness (A): 0.000 (Na=0; Nc=1)
- Distance (D): 1.000
- Status: Zone of Pain

Scope: One ROS/HoloOcean adapter class with sensor conversion helpers and a synchronous simulation loop. Framework dependence is expected here; missing internal seams and lifecycle mixing are the issues.

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | SRP | `sim_bridge/sim_bridge/holoocean_node.py:59` / `HoloOceanROS2Bridge` | **Moderate.** One class loads scenario JSON, initializes middleware, owns command state, steps the simulator and encodes every sensor; construction calls the blocking loop at line 136. | Simulator lifecycle, deployment configuration and ROS sensor-schema actors change the same class; construction and use cannot be tested independently. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | OCP | `sim_bridge/sim_bridge/holoocean_node.py:478` / `sensor dispatch in _run_holoocean_loop` | **Moderate.** The loop enumerates sensor names and per-sensor try/publish branches; a new sensor requires editing the central stepping loop as well as implementing conversion. | Sensor extension changes simulator scheduling infrastructure; a registry of sensor adapters would localize those changes. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | CCP / DIP (protocol boundary) | `sim_bridge/sim_bridge/holoocean_node.py:122` / `command vector / cmd_callback` | **Moderate.** The shared default eight-element command and Float32MultiArray input go directly to env.step at line 475, sharing the controller’s platform-specific index convention. | Vehicle command-layout changes require coordinated bridge/controller edits; define command values and perform simulator-specific translation at the adapter boundary. |
| <a id="v-04"></a>V-04 | REP (dependency metadata) | `sim_bridge/package.xml:11` / `dependency declarations` | **Moderate.** Manifest has only test dependencies although runtime imports rclpy, ROS message packages, cv_bridge, NumPy and HoloOcean (main); setup.py declares only setuptools. | The installed component metadata does not state the runtime prerequisites needed by deployment consumers. |
| <a id="v-05"></a>V-05 | SRP / DIP | `sim_bridge/sim_bridge/holoocean_node.py:548` / `vortex_field / set_ocean_currents` | **Moderate.** Sensor handling also computes an experiment-specific vortex field and directly applies it to the hardcoded auv0 simulator agent at line 549. | Environmental experiment policy shares the sensor transport/stepping loop; inject CurrentField and scenario-owned agent selection so transport changes do not alter experimental conditions. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: `SimulationSession` (step/close), `CurrentField`, `CommandSource`, narrow per-sensor output sinks and `ScenarioLoader`; pure SensorFrame/Pose/Command values.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Retain injected holoocean_make_fn and separate construction from running. Isolate scenario loading, simulator stepping and ROS sensor encoding; register sensor adapters outside the step loop.

## Ideal Clean Internal Package Diagram

This is a proposed partition, not an implemented structure. Ports are policy-owned abstract interfaces (Python Protocol/ABC, C++ pure virtual classes); implementations depend on them. Entities cross ports as plain values. A grouped port box means separate role-specific interfaces, never one mandatory aggregate interface.

## Principle Assessment and Non-Findings

holoocean_make_fn injection already supports a test seam, so the report does not claim a direct hardcoded simulator constructor inside the class. Driver/framework use in this outer adapter is not a Dependency Rule violation by itself. The superclass Node contract was not shown to promise nonblocking constructors; therefore constructor blocking is reported as SRP/lifecycle coupling, not an invented LSP violation. No scoped ADP/SDP edge or confirmed ISP violation exists; its D=1 does not justify abstracting every sensor helper.

Actor labels are inferred responsibilities, not verified organizational roles. SOLID findings require responsibility, client or contract evidence; line count, inheritance, ordinary guards, framework adapters and missing abstract classes alone do not prove a violation. REP findings establish incomplete reuse/deployment contracts; no release-history audit was performed.

# Package Architecture Audit: motion_control

## Current Metrics

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | SRP / DIP | `motion_control/src/waypoint_control_node.cpp:17` / `WaypointControlNode` | **Critical.** One ROS node owns sensor state, heading/waypoint policy, runtime parameter access, vehicle-specific eight-thruster mixing and command publication (lines 279, 323, 340, 369). | Guidance, vehicle hardware allocation and middleware actors share one class; controller tests cannot substitute output/mixing without reaching into node internals. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | OCP | `motion_control/src/waypoint_control_node.cpp:293` / `mode branches / parse_keys` | **Moderate.** Modes yaw_centerize/full_centerize and key-to-thruster branches at line 369 are hardcoded into the policy class. | Adding a control mode or vehicle layout requires modifying the shared controller; use a selected ControlLaw and platform mixer at the composition boundary. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | SRP / DIP | `motion_control/src/point_iterator.cpp:10` / `PathPlanner` | **Moderate.** Queue progression, initial mission state and waypoint completion are hosted directly inside a ROS Node with transport arrays and timers. | Mission queue behavior and transport changes share one object; a pure queue state machine cannot currently be called independently. |
| <a id="v-04"></a>V-04 | CCP / DIP | `motion_control/src/point_iterator.cpp:53` / `pointSegment_callback wire format` | **Moderate.** Trajectory input uses Float32MultiArray and an implicit four-value waypoint convention, mirrored in python_planner/planner_node.py:_publish_trajectory and controller input. | Trajectory schema changes require coordinated planner/control edits without a shared domain value contract; this is protocol coupling, not an additional source import edge. |
| <a id="v-05"></a>V-05 | CRP / DIP | `motion_control/src/PathPlanning.py:11` / `legacy PathPlanner imports` | **Low.** Unbuilt legacy Python source mutates sys.path to an absolute repository path and imports Tools.explore2D, Tools.mapping2D and routeCalculation outside this component. | Reusing the dormant planner requires repository layout and unrelated helper dependencies; separate or retire it only in a later authorized migration. |
| <a id="v-06"></a>V-06 | REP (dependency metadata) | `motion_control/package.xml:10` / `dependency declarations` | **Moderate.** Manifest contains no dependencies for rclcpp and ROS messages used in the compiled nodes; CMake additionally finds and links nlohmann_json. | Build/release metadata does not describe the component actually installed. The unbuilt placeholder alone is not a SOLID violation. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: `VehicleState`, `WaypointQueue`, `ControlLaw`, `ThrusterMixer`, `CommandSink`, `Clock`; split guidance decisions from platform allocation and ROS serialization.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Move control and queue use cases out of Node subclasses; adapters translate ROS arrays to typed trajectory/report values. Inject a vehicle-specific mixer. Preserve current control behavior during any later extraction.

## Ideal Clean Internal Package Diagram

This is a proposed partition, not an implemented structure. Ports are policy-owned abstract interfaces (Python Protocol/ABC, C++ pure virtual classes); implementations depend on them. ## Principle Assessment and Non-Findings

ADP/SDP refer to the observed deployment graph, not cyclic imports. SAP is supported by concrete MidrunLogger being depended upon by unrelated production clients. ISP applies to those clients depending on its aggregate API, not a claim that subclasses are forced to override methods. REP/CCP/CRP evidence is current dependency/reuse structure; team change history was not available. BenchmarkLogger.destroy_node calls its superclass; IO errors could interrupt cleanup, but a proven base-contract violation has not been established, so no LSP finding is asserted.

Actor labels are inferred responsibilities, not verified organizational roles. SOLID findings require responsibility, client or contract evidence; line count, inheritance, ordinary guards, framework adapters and missing abstract classes alone do not prove a violation. REP findings establish incomplete reuse/deployment contracts; no release-history audit was performed.

### FILE: docs/architecture/packages/bringup_audit.md
# Package Architecture Audit: bringup

## Current Metrics

- Fan-in: 1 | Fan-out: 5
- Instability (I): 0.833
- Abstractness (A): 0.000* (Na=0; Nc=0)
- Distance (D): 0.167*
- Status: Zone of Pain*

Scope: Composition-only Python ROS package: launch wiring and configuration resources, no production classes. Concrete framework references are appropriate at this outer boundary.

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | ADP | `bringup/launch/mapper.launch.py:77` / `benchmark observer launch` | **Critical.** Launch depends on benchmark_evaluator while its optimizer launches this same bringup component; perception/planner launch paths also close the longer telemetry cycles. | Production composition is cyclically coupled to experiment orchestration. Launching an observer is legitimate; placing the reverse launcher in that observer release causes the cycle. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | REP (dependency metadata) | `bringup/package.xml:11` / `dependency declarations` | **Moderate.** Manifest has test dependencies but none for launch, launch_ros, ament_index_python or the five launched scoped components. | A consumer installing bringup cannot rely on the declared closure to obtain the executables it orchestrates. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | CCP | `bringup/launch/mapper.launch.py:11` / `configuration resource contract` | **Moderate.** The launch package owns ROS configuration files whose exact node keys and source-tree layout are read and rewritten by benchmark_evaluator/optuna_optimizer.py:12 and :23. | Deployment renames and research parameter injection require coordinated edits across two components; configuration translation needs one owner. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: No abstract wrapper around LaunchDescription is needed. Extract a plain LaunchSpec/ExperimentConfig contract for the external experiment runner; keep policy-owned parameter objects in their policy components.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Keep deployment composition depending on adapters and use-case factories. Move optimizer-specific invocation and interpretation into the experiment root; declare all launched packages and launch tools.

```mermaid
 LD --> N[launch_ros.Node]

```

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | SRP / DIP | `active_inspection_planner/src/realtime_stereo_mapper.hpp:26` / `RealTimeStereoMapper` | **Critical.** One class owns map insertion and storage, camera projection, frontier selection, route ordering and trajectory smoothing; its public process_frame API takes cv::Mat and private state is a concrete ColorOcTree (lines 30, 51). | Mapping/backend maintainers and mission researchers change the same class; policy cannot be exercised independently of mapping/image libraries. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | DIP | `active_inspection_planner/src/realtime_stereo_mapper.cpp:119` / `generate_candidate_viewpoints` | **Moderate.** Viewpoint policy traverses concrete OctoMap leaves at line 134 and builds PCL search structures; map access has no policy-owned query contract. | A map representation change propagates into utility scoring and candidate planning. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | SRP / DIP | `active_inspection_planner/src/realtime_stereo_mapper.cpp:112` / `save_map` | **Moderate.** The mapping/planning object also writes the on-disk map and reports through stdout; process_frame embeds camera mount calibration at line 73. | Persistence and vehicle-calibration changes share a compilation unit with mission policy. |
| <a id="v-04"></a>V-04 | SRP / DIP | `active_inspection_planner/src/nbv_utility_node.cpp:15` / `NBVUtilityNode` | **Moderate.** ROS callbacks and timer implement mission progress/termination and map-save behavior while directly constructing RealTimeStereoMapper at line 31. | Mission-policy tests require framework lifecycle or extracted methods; middleware integration and mission research share ownership. |
| <a id="v-05"></a>V-05 | CCP / CRP | `active_inspection_planner/CMakeLists.txt:21` / `nbv_utility_node target` | **Moderate.** Mapper, route search and ROS entry point are compiled as one executable with no separately reusable policy target. | Search-only reuse inherits the mapping/ROS component release; backend and algorithm changes travel together. |
| <a id="v-06"></a>V-06 | REP (dependency metadata) | `active_inspection_planner/package.xml:11` / `dependency declarations` | **Moderate.** Manifest omits directly used rclcpp, sensor_msgs, std_msgs, nav_msgs, Eigen and PCL dependencies; compare CMakeLists.txt:14 and nbv_utility_node.cpp:1. | Build/release consumers cannot reconstruct the component dependency closure from its manifest. This is release-contract evidence, not a claim about historical versioning. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: `MapReader`, `MapWriter`, `MapSnapshotStore`, `CandidateSelector`, `RouteSearch`, `TrajectoryOutput`, `MissionTelemetry`; each role has a narrow pure virtual boundary using value types.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Move OctoMap/PCL/OpenCV behind mapping adapters. Let mission use cases consume map snapshots and injected route search. Keep ROS startup in the composition root and file serialization in an output adapter.

### FILE: docs/architecture/packages/python_planner_audit.md
# Package Architecture Audit: python_planner

## Current Metrics

- Fan-in: 1 | Fan-out: 1
- Instability (I): 0.500
- Abstractness (A): 0.000 (Na=0; Nc=3)
- Distance (D): 0.500
- Status: Zone of Pain

Scope: Active NBV ROS planner with separate scoring, route and continuity functions, plus a legacy Tools mapping pipeline. Three concrete classes, no explicit abstract interfaces.

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | SRP / DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/planner_node.py:58` / `NBVUtilityNode` | **Critical.** A single Node contains sensor conversion, map fusion, mission progression, target blacklisting, collision replanning, trajectory serialization and file persistence. Constructor creates OctoMap at line 155, MidrunLogger at 164 and evaluation file at 192. | Mission research, mapping, ROS integration and experiment reporting actors share one class; failures/changes cascade across policy and IO. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/get_NBV.py:4` / `get_logger / calculate_nbv_waypoints` | **Moderate.** The NBV algorithm imports a ROS logger and constructs/filters Open3D point clouds inside policy (line 262); scoring helpers also take Open3D point-cloud objects. | Pure candidate/scoring use cases require framework/preprocessing dependencies even outside ROS; inject diagnostics and preprocessing output. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | DIP / CRP / ADP | `python_planner/python_planner/planner_node.py:19` / `MidrunLogger dependency` | **Moderate.** Mission code imports concrete experiment telemetry and accesses its filter_path (line 200), while benchmark orchestration depends back on the bringup graph. | A logging implementation and result naming convention become production dependencies and participate in a deployment cycle. |
| <a id="v-04"></a>V-04 | SRP / DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/Tools/get_point_cloud.py:66` / `legacy RealTimeStereoMapper` | **Moderate.** One class combines map fusion, candidate/path policy, direct Notes file writes, map persistence and visualization methods; constructor opens a file at 69 and creates ColorOcTree at 77. | Map/backend, display and algorithm actors modify one legacy implementation; it cannot serve as an independent domain mapper. |
| <a id="v-05"></a>V-05 | REP / DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/Tools/mapping_3d.py:10` / `legacy Tools import routing` | **Low.** Absolute sys.path mutation plus top-level Tools imports bypass installed python_planner ownership. Tools/get_point_cloud.py:11 and Tools/mapping3D.py:8 use the same ambiguous namespace. | Reuse depends on checkout layout and may resolve a different repository Tools module. No out-of-scope implementation is assumed to be the intended provider. |
| <a id="v-06"></a>V-06 | CCP / CRP | `python_planner/python_planner/Tools/mapping3D.py:22` / `parallel legacy planning pipeline` | **Moderate.** Legacy route/search/mapping implementations coexist with the active route_planning/get_NBV/Node pipeline in one source component, using distinct data/calibration and IO assumptions. | Consumers and maintainers must distinguish two change paths for the same capability; dormant code is not evidence that active mission execution calls it. |
| <a id="v-07"></a>V-07 | DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/trajectory_optimization.py:139` / `optimize_trajectory` | **Moderate.** Trajectory acceptance policy directly invokes scipy.optimize.minimize with a fixed solver and options; no injectable optimization operation separates objective/validation from solver mechanics. | Replacing/timing/faking the solver requires modifying or monkey-patching policy. Retain collision and improvement checks in the use case, not the solver adapter. |
| <a id="v-08"></a>V-08 | CCP / DIP | `python_planner/python_planner/planner_node.py:422` / `_publish_trajectory` | **Moderate.** Policy layer performs Float32 wire serialization/validation while control interprets an implicit x,y,z,yaw array convention; entity and adapter concerns meet inside the mission class. | Changing command precision/schema requires coordinated planner/control edits. Wire-safety checks remain necessary but belong at an explicit adapter contract. |
| <a id="v-09"></a>V-09 | REP (dependency metadata) | `python_planner/package.xml:16` / `runtime dependencies` | **Moderate.** Active planner imports pyoctomap at planner_node.py:8 but the manifest and setup.py do not declare it; legacy modules add undeclared plotting/Pandas dependencies. | Declared package installation is insufficient to reproduce all shipped runtime imports. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: `OccupancyReader`, `MapIntegrator`, `MapSnapshotStore`, `TrajectoryOutput`, `NBVTelemetry`, `Clock`, `PointCloudPreprocessor`, `TrajectoryOptimizer`; data contracts for Pose, CameraCalibration, Candidate, Trajectory and MissionState.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Extract mission transitions from Node callbacks; adapt ROS messages at entry/output. Inject map and persistence ports and supply telemetry from outer composition. Keep collision checks and continuity acceptance in policy even when an optimizer implementation is replaced.

## Ideal Clean Internal Package Diagram

This is a proposed partition, not an implemented structure. Ports are policy-owned abstract interfaces (Python Protocol/ABC, C++ pure virtual classes); implementations depend on them. Entities cross ports as plain values. A grouped port box means separate role-specific interfaces, never one mandatory aggregate interface.

```mermaid
graph LR

 S[SciPy optimizer implementation]
 end
 subgraph UseCases[Use Cases]
 U[UpdateMap / ChooseView / PlanAndValidate / ReplanMission]
 P[MapReader / MapIntegrator / SnapshotStore / TrajectoryOutput / Telemetry / Optimizer interfaces]
 end
 subgraph Entities
 E[Pose / Calibration / Candidate / Trajectory / MissionState]
 A[Collision / frontier / route / continuity rules]
 end
 R --> U
 U --> P
 O -. implements .-> P
 F -. implements .-> P
 S -. implements .-> P
 U --> A
 A --> E
 P --> E
```

## Principle Assessment and Non-Findings

Framework imports in actual policy are DIP findings; NumPy arithmetic and pure route functions are not automatically violations. The existing timing_callback in calculate_nbv_waypoints is a useful dependency-inversion seam despite the remaining global ROS logger. No strategy subclass contract failure is demonstrated (LSP), and no implemented fat abstract interface proves ISP. Parameterized weights already support extension without editing scoring; merely having conditional collision guards does not violate OCP. SAP: lack of stable policy contracts matters qualitatively; D=0.5 alone is insufficient evidence.

Actor labels are inferred responsibilities, not verified organizational roles. SOLID findings require responsibility, client or contract evidence; line count, inheritance, ordinary guards, framework adapters and missing abstract classes alone do not prove a violation. REP findings establish incomplete reuse/deployment contracts; no release-history audit was performed.

# Package Architecture Audit: perception

## Current Metrics

- Fan-in: 1 | Fan-out: 1
- Instability (I): 0.500
- Abstractness (A): 0.000 (Na=0; Nc=20)
- Distance (D): 0.500
- Status: Zone of Pain

Scope: ROS stereo depth adapter, eager global inference module and vendored BGNet research code (20 concrete classes total). BGNet is a pinned Git submodule with its own upstream provenance, but has no separate package.xml or setup.py release unit here; its Python subpackages are measured inside the enclosing ROS component.

Counts use the observed source/deployment graph, not ROS topic flow. See [Phase 1 definitions and denominator conventions](../phase_1_topology.md#metric-definitions-and-counting-rules). An asterisk denotes a mathematically undefined denominator replaced by zero only for display; the resulting D/status is conditional. Classification alone is not a violation. All file paths below are relative to `NBV_paper_ros2/src`.

## Violations Found
| ID | Rule Broken | File & Line / Symbol | Concrete Issue | Actor / Architectural Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="v-01"></a>V-01 | DIP / SRP | `perception/perception/compute_depth.py:15` / `global inference initialization` | **Critical.** Importing compute_stereo_depth constructs BGNet_Plus, chooses the device, loads a concrete checkpoint and configures transforms at module scope (lines 18–24). | Depth computation cannot be imported without model-loading infrastructure; inference research, deployment paths and hardware startup share module lifecycle. |
| <a id="v-02"></a>V-02 | REP / DIP | `perception/perception/compute_depth.py:7` / `sys.path and checkpoint paths` | **Moderate.** Absolute workstation sys.path and model-file paths resolve top-level models/datasets imports outside the normal installed perception namespace. | Installation/reuse depends on source checkout layout; model packaging and application startup are coupled. |
| <a id="v-03"></a>V-03 | SRP / DIP | `perception/perception/depth_estimation.py:61` / `sync_cam_callback` | **Moderate.** ROS callback converts images, applies camera-specific inference arguments, invokes the concrete depth function, records file-backed telemetry and publishes output; logger is constructed at line 29. | ROS synchronization, model selection/calibration and experiment logging cannot change independently at a use-case boundary. |
| <a id="v-04"></a>V-04 | DIP / CRP / ADP | `perception/perception/depth_estimation.py:13` / `MidrunLogger import` | **Moderate.** Perception depends on benchmark_evaluator only to obtain a timing writer, pulling an experiment component into runtime depth processing and closing a deployment cycle through bringup. | Logging/storage changes and experiment component packaging affect the perception release. |
| <a id="v-05"></a>V-05 | CCP / CRP | `perception/setup.py:8` / `find_packages and BGNet subtree` | **Moderate.** Runtime ROS inference and vendored model variants, dataset classes and research prediction utilities occupy the same package tree/release boundary. | Consumers needing only depth inference inherit changes for dataset/research tooling they do not reuse. Keep framework model internals cohesive behind an adapter. |
| <a id="v-06"></a>V-06 | OCP | `perception/perception/BGNet/predict.py:36` / `model and dataset selection` | **Low.** Model selection and output-folder selection use literal if/elif branches; adding a model or dataset requires editing the prediction script in addition to model/dataset definitions. | Research variant authors modify the central runner; a registry or injected factory can localize extension. CLI composition is a low-severity boundary, not a demand for polymorphism in every branch. |
| <a id="v-07"></a>V-07 | REP (dependency metadata) | `perception/package.xml:11` / `dependency declarations` | **Moderate.** Manifest declares benchmark_evaluator and message_filters but omits rclpy, sensor/std messages, cv_bridge, NumPy, OpenCV, Torch, torchvision and PIL used by runtime source; setup.py declares only setuptools. | The release unit does not communicate its runtime installation requirements. |

## Proposed Structural Boundary
- Interfaces to extract: `StereoDepthEstimator`, `DepthOutput`, `PipelineTimingSink`, `ModelLoader`; pass StereoPair, CameraCalibration and DepthFrame values through a client-owned boundary.
- Dependencies to invert or decouple: Construct BGNet/checkpoint/device adapters explicitly at startup. Keep ROS synchronization outside depth policy and reuse only narrow telemetry contracts. Isolate vendored research dataset and prediction utilities from runtime inference packaging.

## Current Internal Package Diagram

Solid arrows are calls, construction, field ownership or source dependencies as labeled. These diagrams summarize source relationships, not measured runtime execution; dormant paths are labeled. Red nodes mix policy with details.

## Ideal Clean Internal Package Diagram

This is a proposed partition, not an implemented structure. Ports are policy-owned abstract interfaces (Python Protocol/ABC, C++ pure virtual classes); implementations depend on them. Entities cross ports as plain values. A grouped port box means separate role-specific interfaces, never one mandatory aggregate interface.

```mermaid
graph LR
 subgraph Adapters[Interface Adapters]
 R[ROS synchronization and CvBridge]
 B[BGNet adapter: Torch / model loader / preprocessor]
 T[File timing adapter]
 CLI[Separate research dataset and prediction entry points]
 end
 subgraph UseCases[Use Cases]
 U[EstimateStereoDepth]
 P[StereoDepthEstimator / TimingSink / DepthOutput interfaces]
 end
 subgraph Entities
 E[StereoPair / CameraCalibration / DepthFrame / StageTiming]
 end
 R --> U
 U --> P
 B -. implements .-> P
 T -. implements .-> P
 CLI --> B
 P --> E
 U --> E
```

## Principle Assessment and Non-Findings

PyTorch models legitimately depend on Torch as outer inference implementations; those imports are not independently labeled DIP violations. Dataset and Module subclasses show no demonstrated substitution-contract violation. The inconsistent dataset load_path tuple arity and conditionally defined get_last_depth_timings are ordinary contract/correctness concerns, recorded separately rather than mislabeled LSP. No abstract fat interface is implemented here; the concrete logger dependency is assessed under benchmark ISP. ADP membership comes from deployment plus telemetry, not BGNet cyclic imports.

Actor labels are inferred responsibilities, not verified organizational roles. SOLID findings require responsibility, client or contract evidence; line count, inheritance, ordinary guards, framework adapters and missing abstract classes alone do not prove a violation. REP findings establish incomplete reuse/deployment contracts; no release-history audit was performed.

### FILE: active_inspection_planner/src/nbv_utility_node.cpp
#include <rclcpp/rclcpp.hpp>
#include <sensor_msgs/msg/image.hpp>
#include <sensor_msgs/msg/magnetic_field.hpp>
#include <nav_msgs/msg/odometry.hpp>
#include <std_msgs/msg/float32_multi_array.hpp>
#include <std_msgs/msg/bool.hpp>
#include <cv_bridge/cv_bridge.hpp>
#include <tf2/LinearMath/Quaternion.h>
#include <tf2/LinearMath/Matrix3x3.h>

#include "active_inspection_planner/use_cases/mission.hpp"
#include "active_inspection_planner/adapters/maps.hpp"
#include "active_inspection_planner/adapters/mission_io.hpp"
#include <fstream>
#include <chrono>

class NBVUtilityNode : public rclcpp::Node {
public:
    NBVUtilityNode() : Node("nbv_utility_node") {
        const inspection::MapperConfig cfg{90.0,256.0,0.5,
            declare_parameter("voxel_resolution",0.1),declare_parameter("max_depth",5.0),
            declare_parameter("min_depth",0.5),declare_parameter("z_min_target",-32.0),
            declare_parameter("info_gain_threshold",0.05),declare_parameter("sparsity_multiplier",0.8),
            declare_parameter("min_utility_threshold",0.005)};
        // QoS for sensor data
        rclcpp::QoS qos(10);
        qos.best_effort();

        // Subscribers
        depth_sub_ = this->create_subscription<sensor_msgs::msg::Image>(
            "holocean/depth/image_raw", qos, 
            std::bind(&NBVUtilityNode::depth_callback, this, std::placeholders::_1));

        rgb_sub_ = this->create_subscription<sensor_msgs::msg::Image>(
            "holocean/cameraDown/image_raw", qos, 
            std::bind(&NBVUtilityNode::rgb_callback, this, std::placeholders::_1));

        odom_sub_ = this->create_subscription<nav_msgs::msg::Odometry>(
            "holocean/odom", qos, 
            std::bind(&NBVUtilityNode::odom_callback, this, std::placeholders::_1));

        mag_sub_ = this->create_subscription<sensor_msgs::msg::MagneticField>(
            "holocean/mag", qos, 
            std::bind(&NBVUtilityNode::mag_callback, this, std::placeholders::_1));

        // Publishers
        waypoint_pub_ = this->create_publisher<std_msgs::msg::Float32MultiArray>("object/areaWaypoints", 10);
        finished_pub_ = this->create_publisher<std_msgs::msg::Bool>("/movement/finished_execution", 10);

        diagnostics_ = std::make_unique<inspection::RosDiagnostics>(get_logger());
        map_ = std::make_unique<inspection::OctomapAdapter>(cfg.resolution,*diagnostics_);
        update_ = std::make_unique<inspection::MapUpdate>(cfg,*map_,*map_);
        route_ = std::make_unique<inspection::RoutePlanner>(cfg.resolution,*diagnostics_);
        candidates_ = std::make_unique<inspection::CandidatePlanner>(cfg,*map_,indexes_,*route_,*diagnostics_);
        output_ = std::make_unique<inspection::RosMissionOutput>(waypoint_pub_,finished_pub_);
        mission_ = std::make_unique<inspection::InspectionMission>(*update_,*candidates_,*map_,*map_,
            *output_,metrics_,clock_,*diagnostics_,
            "/home/metin-ege/AIEngineering/RoboticFocus/HoloSystem/NBV_paper_ros2/final_map_ros2.bt");
        timer_=create_wall_timer(std::chrono::milliseconds(500),[this]{mission_->timer_callback();});
        RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "✅ Object Detector Node Started in C++");
    }
private:
    inspection::PclIndexes indexes_;
    inspection::SystemClock clock_;
    inspection::FileMissionMetrics metrics_{"evaluation_parameters.txt"};
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::RosDiagnostics> diagnostics_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::OctomapAdapter> map_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::MapUpdate> update_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::RoutePlanner> route_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::CandidatePlanner> candidates_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::RosMissionOutput> output_;
    std::unique_ptr<inspection::InspectionMission> mission_;
    rclcpp::Subscription<sensor_msgs::msg::Image>::SharedPtr depth_sub_, rgb_sub_;
    rclcpp::Subscription<nav_msgs::msg::Odometry>::SharedPtr odom_sub_;
    rclcpp::Subscription<sensor_msgs::msg::MagneticField>::SharedPtr mag_sub_;
    rclcpp::Publisher<std_msgs::msg::Float32MultiArray>::SharedPtr waypoint_pub_;
    rclcpp::Publisher<std_msgs::msg::Bool>::SharedPtr finished_pub_;
    rclcpp::TimerBase::SharedPtr timer_;


    void odom_callback(const nav_msgs::msg::Odometry::SharedPtr msg) {
        Eigen::Vector3d position(msg->pose.pose.position.x,msg->pose.pose.position.y,msg->pose.pose.position.z);
        if(!mission_->accepts_pose(position)) return;
        const auto& o=msg->pose.pose.orientation;
        tf2::Quaternion q(o.x,o.y,o.z,o.w); tf2::Matrix3x3 m(q);
        double roll,pitch,yaw; m.getRPY(roll,pitch,yaw);
        mission_->pose(inspection::Pose{position,yaw,pitch,roll});
    }
    void mag_callback(const sensor_msgs::msg::MagneticField::SharedPtr msg) {
        mission_->magnetic(msg->magnetic_field.x,msg->magnetic_field.y);
    }
    void rgb_callback(const sensor_msgs::msg::Image::SharedPtr msg) {
        mission_->rgb(inspection::color_values(cv_bridge::toCvCopy(msg,"bgr8")->image));
    }
    void depth_callback(const sensor_msgs::msg::Image::SharedPtr msg) {
        mission_->depth(inspection::depth_values(cv_bridge::toCvCopy(msg,msg->encoding)->image));
    }
};
int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    rclcpp::init(argc, argv);
    rclcpp::spin(std::make_shared<NBVUtilityNode>());
    rclcpp::shutdown();
    return 0;
}
